जहा कोई आयकर परिमंडल , कई अथा . जिला अथव . रेंज या उसका 
कोई भाग हम अधिसूचना द्वारा एक रेंज में किमी अन्य रेज में अन्तरिम कर 
दिया गया हो , वहां उस आयकर परिमहल , ४ अथय जिलः अथवा रेंज 
या उसके किमी भाग में फियं गये निर्धारणी में उत्पन्न होने वाली और हम 
अधिसूचना का मार्ग में तत्काल पूर्व, रेंज के उम अ यकर आयुक्त ( अपील ) 
के ममक्ष विचार धान परी अपीले, जिमने अधिकार- क्षेत्र में उक्त आयकर 
परिमंडल जिला, वार्ड अथवा रेज या उसका कोई भाग अन्नग्नि किया गया 
हो इस अधिसूचना के लागू होने की न रख में रेज के उस आयकर आ 
युक्त ( अपोल ) को अंतरित को आएगी और उसके द्वारा निपटायी जाएगी 
जिमके अधिकार क्षेत्र में उमस मोई परिमाइल , जिम्ना अथवा रेज या उमका 
कोई भाग अन्मरित किया गया है । 
यह अधिसूचना 1 - 9-1985 मे लग होगी ।

S . O . 4937. - -- Whercas by notification of the Govern 
ment of India in the Ministry of Petroleum S .O . 3940 
datod 7 -8 -85 under sub - section ( 1 ) of Section 3 of 
the Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right 
of User in Land) Act , 1962 ( 50 of 1962 ) , the Central Govern 
ment declared its intention to acquire the right of user in 
the lands specified in the Schedule appended to that notifica 
tion for purpose of laying pipeline ; 


का . प्रा . 49१५.- ~-यत: पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइप लाइन ( मि 
में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्गन ) अधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) 
क धारा 3 क उपधारा ( 1 ) के अध न मारत सरकार के पेट्रोलियम मंत्रालय 
को मधिसूचना का प्रा . म , 3966 तारीख 7 - 8 -65 द्वारा 
केन्द्र य सरकार ने उस अधिसूचना से मंलग्न अनुसूच मे विनिर्दिष्ट भूमियों 
के उपयोग के अधिकार को पाइप लाइनों को बिछाने के लिये अजित करने 
का अपमा पाय घोषित कर दिया था । 


And whereas the Competent Authority has under sub 
section ( 1 ) of Section 6 of the said Act, submitted report to 
the Government ; 


मौर यतः सक्षम प्राधिकारी ने उम्त अधिनियम क धारा 6क उपधारा 
( 1 ) के अधीन सरकार को रिपोर्ट दे दी है । 


And further whereas the Central Government has, after 
considering the said rcport, decided to acquire the right of 
piger in the lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notification; 


पौर प्रागे , यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उक्त रिपोर्ट पर विचार करने के 
पश्चात् इस अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट भूमियों में उपयोग 
का अधिकार मजित करने का विनिश्चय किया है । 

अब , अत . उक्त अधिनियम कः धारा 6 मा . उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा 
प्रदत्त शक्ति का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्र य सरकार एमद्वारा घोधित करती 
है कि इस अधिसूचना में मनग्न अनुसूच में विनिर्दिष्ट उक्त भूमियों में उप 
योन का अधिकार पाइप लाइन बिछाने के प्रयोजन के लिये एतदद्वाग 
अजित किया जाता है । 


Now , therefore , in exercise of the power conferred by 
Sub-section ( 1 ) of Section 6 of the said Act , the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of usce in the said 
tands specified in the schedule appended to this notification 
hereby acquired for laying the pipeline ;

S. O . 4939. - Whereas by notification of the Govern 
ment of India in the Ministry of Petroleum, S . O . 3868 dated 
7 - 8 - 85 under sub -section ( 1 ) of Section 3 of the Petroleum 
and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in Land ) , 
Act , 1962 (50 of 1962) , the Contral Government 
declared its intention to acquire the right of user in the 
lands specified in the schedule appendod to that notification 
for the purpose of laying pipeling ; 


और आगे यलः केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उक्त रिपोर्ट पर विचार 
भारने के पश्चात् इस अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची में 
विनिर्दिष्ट भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने का 
विनिश्चय किया है । 

अब , अत:, उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 6 की उपधारा 
( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्ति का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार 
एसद्वारा घोषित करती है कि इस अधिसूचना में संलग्न 
अनुसूची में विनिदिष्ट उक्त भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार 
पाइपलाईन बिछाने के प्रयोजन के लिये एतदद्वारा अजित 
किया जाता है । 

और आगे उक्त धारा की उपधारा ( 4 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त 
शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार निर्देश देती 
है कि उक्त भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार केन्द्रीय सरकार 
में निहित होने के बजाय भारतीय गैस प्राधिकरण लि० में 
समी बाधाओं से मुक्त रूप से घोषणा के प्रकाशन की इस 
तारीख से निहित होगा ।

to the Government ;

considering the said report, decided to acquire tho right of 
uger in the lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notification ;

8ub -section ( 1 ) of Section 6 of the said Act, the Central

का . मा . 4943. - यतः पेट्रोलियम और खनिज 
पाइपलाइन ( भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन ) अधि 
नियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50) की धारा 3 की उपधारा 
( 1) के अधीन भारत सरकार के पेट्रोलियम मंत्रालय की 
अधिसूचना का . आ . सं . 1011 तारीख 25- 2- 88 द्वारा 
केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उस अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची में 
विनिर्दिष्ट भूमियों के उपयोग के अधिकार को पाइप लाइनों 
को विछाने के लिए मजित करने का अपना आशय घोषित 
भार दिया था । 

और यतः सक्षम प्राधिकारी ने उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 
6 की उपधारा ( 1 ) के अधीन सरकार को रिपोर्ट दे दी है । 

और आगे यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उक्त रिपोर्ट पर विचार 
करने के पश्चात् इस अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची में विनि 
विष्ट भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार अजित मारने का 
विनिश्चय किया है । 
__ अब , अत: उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 6 की उपधारा 
( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्ति का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार 
एतदद्वारा घोषित करती है कि इस अधिसूचना में संलग्न अनुसूची में 
विनिर्दिष्ट उक्त भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार पाइपलाइन 
बिछाने के प्रयोजन के लिए एसद्वारा अजित किया जाता है । 
__ और मागे उस धारा की उपधारा ( 4 ) द्वारा प्रवत्त 
शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकारनिर्देश देतो है कि 
उक्त भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार केन्द्रीय सरकार में निहित होने 
के बजाय भारतीय गैस प्राधिकरण लि . में सभी बाधाओं से मुक्त 
रूप में घोषणा के प्रकाशन की इस तारीख को निहित होगा ।

१००4944. न्यत: पैट्रोलियम मोर बनिण पाइपलाईन ( भूमि में 
उपयोग के अधिकार का मर्जन ) अधिनियम , 1982 ( 1982 का 50 ) की 
पारा 3 की उपधारा ( 1 ) के अधीन भारत सरकार के पैट्रोलियम मंत्रालय 
की अधिसपना का मा० सं० 1830 तारोष 13- 4-88 
द्वारा केन्द्रीय सरकार मे उस अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची में 
विनिर्षिष्ट भूमियों के उपयोग के अधिकार को पाइपलाइनों को बिछाने के 
लिये प्रणित करने का अपना माशय पोषित कर दिया पा । 

पौर यतः सक्षम प्राधिकारी ने उक्त प्रधिनियम की धारा की 
उपधारा ( 1 ) के अधीन सरकार को रिपोर्ट दे दी है । । 

पौर मागे यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उक्त रिपोर्ट पर विचार करने के 
पश्चात् इस अधिसूचना से संमग्न अनुसूची में विनिषिष्ट भूमियों में उपयोग 
का अधिकार प्रणित करने का विनिश्चय किया है ।

द्वारा केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उस अधिसूचना ने संलग्न अनुसूची 
में विनिर्दिष्ट भूमियों के उपयोग के अधिकार को पाइप 
लाइनों को बिछाने के लिए अजित करने का अपना प्राशय 
घोषित कर दिया था । 

और, यतः सक्षम प्राधिकारी ने उक्त अधिनियम 
की धारा 6 की उपधारा ( 1 ) के अधीन सरकार को रिपोर्ट 
दे दी है । 

और, प्रागे , यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उक्त रिपोर्ट पर 
विचार परने के पश्चात इस अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची 
में विनिर्दिष्ट भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने 
का विनिश्चय किया है । 

अब , अत : उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 6 की उपधारा 
( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्ति का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार 
एतद्द्वारा घोषित करतो है कि इस अधिसूचना में संलग्न अनुसूची 
में विनिर्दिष्ट उक्त भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार पाइप 
लाइन विछाने के प्रयोजन के लिए एतदद्वारा अजित किया 
जाता है । 

और आगे उस धारा की उपधारा ( 4 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त 
शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार में निहित होने 
के बाग भारतीय गैस प्राधिकरण लि . में सभी बाधाओं 

से मुक्त रूप में घोषणा के प्रकाशन की इस तारीख को 
निहित होगा ।

का . मा . 4946. -- यतः पेट्रोलियम मार खनिज पाइपलाइन ( भूमि 
में उपयोग के अधिकार का प्रर्जन ) अधिनियम 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) 
को धारा 3 क ; उपधारा ( 1 ) के प्रघम भारत सरकार के पेट्रोलियम 
मंत्रालय कं . अधिसूचना का . पा . सं 2091 तारं न 11-5- 85 
द्वारा केन्द्र य सरकार ने उस अधिसूचना में संलग्न पनुसूप में विनिर्दिष्ट 
भूमियों के उपयोग के अधिकार को पाइप लाइनों को बिछाने के लिये 
पजित करने का प्रपना प्राशय पोषित कर दिया था ; 

पौर यत: सक्षम प्राधिकार ने उक्त प्रधिनियम की धारा 6 के उप 
बारा ( 1 ) के भघ न सरकार को रिपोर्ट दे पा है ; 

पौर प्रागे यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उक्त रिपोर्ट पर विचार करने के 
पश्चात् इस अधिसूचना से मंलग्न अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट भूमियों में उपयोग 
का अधिकार अजित करने का विनिश्चय किया है । 

प्रव, अतः उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 6 की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त 
शक्ति का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्र य सरकार एतद्वारा घोषित करती है 
कि इस अधिसूचना में संलग्न अनुसूच में विनिर्दिष्ट उक्त भूमियों में 
उपयोग का अधिकार पाइप लाइन बिछाने प्रयोजन के लिये एसद्वारा 
मजित किया जाता है : 

पौर प्रागे उस धारा क उपधारा ( 4 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का 
प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्र य सरकार में निहित होने के बजाय भारतीय 
गैस प्राधिकरण लि , में सभ बाधामों से मुक्त रूप में घोषणा के प्रकाशन 
को इस तार ब को निहत होगा ।

का० ० 4947.--- यत : पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइप 
लाइन ( भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन ) अधिनियम 
1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3 की उपधारा ( 1 ) 
के अधीन भारत सरकार के पेट्रोलियम मंत्रालय की अधिसूचना 
का० प्रा० . सं० 3211 तारीख 4 - 7- 85 द्वारा केन्द्रीय 
सरकार ने उस अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुमूसी में विनिर्दिष्ट 
भमियों के उपयोग के अधिकार को पाइप लाइनों को 
बिछाने के लिए अजित करने का अपना प्राश्य घोषित कर 
दिया था । 

और यतः सक्षम प्राधिकारी ने उक्त अधिनियम की 
धारा 6 की उपधारा ( 1 ) के अधीन सरकार को रिपोर्ट 
दे दी है । 
____ और पागे यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उक्त रिपोर्ट पर विचार 
करने के पश्चात इस अधिसूचना से संलग्न • अनसूचों 
विनिर्दिष्ट भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने का 
विनिश्चय किया है । 

अब, अतः उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 6 की उपधारा ( 1 ) 
द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्ति का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार एतद्द्वारा 
घोषित करता है कि इस अधिसूचना में संलग्न अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट 
उक्त भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार पाइप लाइन बिछाने के 
प्रयोजन के लिए एतदद्वारा प्रजित किया जाता है । 
____ और आगे उस धारा की उपधारा ( 4 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त 
शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार निर्देश 
देती है कि उक्त भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार 
केन्द्रीय सरकार में निहित होने के बजाय भारतीय गैस 
प्राधिकरण लि० में सभी बाधाओं से मुक्त रूप में घोषणा के 
प्रकाशन की इस तारीख को निहित होगा ।

और मागे यत : केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उक्त रिपोर्ट पर विचार 
कारने के पश्चात इस अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची में 
विनिर्दिष्ट भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने का 
विनिश्चय किया है । 

अब , अत : उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 6 की उपधारा ( 1 ) 
द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्ति का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार 
एतदद्वारा घोषित करती है कि इस अधिसूचना में संलग्न अनुसूची 
में विनिर्दिष्ट उक्त भमियो में उपयोग का अधिकार पाइप लाइन 
विछाने के प्रयोजन के लिए एतद्वारा जित किया जाता है । 

और भागे उस धारा को उपधारा ( 4 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त 
णक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार निर्देश 
देती है : उक्त भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार 
केन्द्रीय सरकार में निहित होने के बजाय भारतीय गैस 
प्राधिकरण लि . में सभी बाधाओं से मुक्त रूप में घोषणा के 
प्राशन को इस तारीख को निहित होगा ।

आ . 3936 तारीख , 7- 8- 85 द्वारा केन्द्रीय सरकार में

6 की उपधारा ( 1 ) के अधीन सरकार को रिपोर्ट 
दे दी है ;

में विनिर्दिष्ट भूमियों में उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने

10 ( 1 )(L ) of the I, D . Act, 1947. 
PARTIES : Employers in relation to the management of 

Loyabad Coke Plant of Messrs, Bharat Coking Coal 
Limited and their workmen . 


The two concerned workmen submitted their explanation 
to the charge sheets issued to them . The explanation was 
found to be unsatisfactory and thereafter an order was 
made appointing Sri S . Kumar , Senior Personnel Officer, 
Sijua Arca office as the Enquiry Officer . The Enquiry Officer 
with due notice to the concerned workmen held the enquiry on 
different dates. In the said domestic enquiry the witnesses for 
thic management deposed at the beck and call of the mange . 
mçnt. Even then the management s Witnesses hopelossly 
contradicted theniselves and did not fully support the case 
of the management. The domestic enquiry was not fair 
And prorer as the Enquiry Officer did not give proper op 
portunity to the concerned workmen to defend themselves . 
Tlie concerned workmen submitted written note of argu 
Tient for consideration of the Enquiry Officer but not of 
those points raised wore considerod in the enquiry re 
port . The Enquiry Oficer did not consider the poMinent

were dismissed from service with immcdiate ülect by an

was held in accordance with the principles oi natural jus 
tice and all oprortunities were given to the concerned work 
ren to defend themselves . Thy action of the nanagement 
ill dismissing ihe concerned workmen is fully justified and 
üs such the concerned workmen are not entiiled to any

circumstances which appeared at the time of enquiry and 
did not draw proper conclusion arising out of those cir 
cumstances. The Enquiry Officer submitted his enquiry 
report" under the influence of the management rendering 
the enquiry report as completely perverse . The allegd oc 
curence on the basis of which the chargesheets were issued 
is completely fabricated story in order to victimise the con 
cerned workmen for their trade union activities. The 
union and their active members were simply present 
for grant of casual leave for those members of the union 
who had gone on hunger strike for non -fulfilment of the 
demands of the union . There was no disorderly behavi 
our on the part of the union and no attempt was made 
on behalf of the workmen either to assault or to abuse 
Shri R . C . Ram P . O . The chargesheet is concentrated on 
the question of personal abuse hurled by the concerned 
workmen on the Personnal Officer Shri R . C . Ram . It is 
alleged that the concerned workmen committed offence 
under clause 10 ( 1 ) , 10 ( 2 ) and 10 ( 8 ) of the Standing 
Orders of the management. Clause 10 ( 1 ) of the Stand 
ing Orders refers to wilful insubordinate or disobedience of 
lawful or a reasonable order of the superior. The said 
allegation does not show that any order was issued by any 
authority which was disobeyed by the workmen . With 
regard to clause 10 ( 2 ) of the Standing Orders the subs 
tance of the clause is that workmen should not resort to 
strike nor they should incite others to resort to strike. At 
the time of domestic enquiry no evidence was adduced 
to the effect that the concerned workmen along with others 
had struck work . The domestic enquiry reveals that some 
of the leaders of the workmen were called in by the 
management for negotiation and discussion of the demands 
and consequently one of the workers came to the office 
of the Manager along with others and they were told by 
the Manager to submit written applications for grant of 
casual leave and thereafter all of the workman went away . 
in order to file the desired application . Thus the clause 10 
( 2 ) of the Standing Orders has no application to the facts 
of the case . Clause 10 ( 2 ) of the Standing order has no apnli 
cation to the effect of the case . Clause 10 ( 2 ) of the standing 
orders refers to drunkness , fighting riotous or disorderly or 
indecent behaviour in the factory premises. The concerned 
workmen were never drunken nor they were fighting nor 
they had indulged in anv indecent behaviour. The evidence 
adduced to prove those allegations on behalf of the manage 
ment cannot be accepted . The Enquiry Officer did not con 
sider the evidence before him and held the concerned work 
men guilty of all the charges. The disciplinary authority 
also accepted the report of the Enauiry Officer and passed 
on orders of dismissal against the concerned workmen , Shri 
R . C . Ram had filed a criminal case before the Chief Ju 
dicial Magistrate which numbered as complaint case No. 
82/ 84 against the concerned workmen under sec . 500 J. P . C . 
But the concerned workmen were acquitted of the charges. 
Shri R . C . Ram had initiated proceeding under Section 107 of 
the Criminal Procedure Code against the concerned work . 
men which was also disposed of . On the above plea it is 
submitted or he alf of the workmen that dismissal of the 
concrned workmen by the management is not justified and 
that they are entitled for reinstatement with back wages 
and continuity of service . 


As the workmen had challenged the fairness and pro 
periety of the domestic enquiry, the manage nent adduc 
ed the evidence of MW - 1 Shri S . Kn : r , S . P . O . who 
had held the enquiry . After the evidence of the E . O ., Shri 
J . P . Singh , learned Advocate appearing on behalf of the 
workmen submitted that he does not challenge the fair 
ness and propriety of the domestic enquiry and that he 
concedes that the domestic enquiry was fair and proper. 
Accordingly it was held that the domestic enquiry held 
against the concerned workmen was fair and proper and the 
Case was heard on merit. 


The management had produced the entire enquiry pro 
ceeding which has been marked as Ext. M - 1 to M - 14 in this 
case . The Standing Order of I oyabad Coke Plant is mark 
ed as Ext. M -15 . The workmen have filed Ext . W - 1 which 
is the order of the criminal court in complaint case No. 
8284 . As the domestic enquiry held against the concerned 
workmen was held to be fair ind proper we have to con 
fine ourselves to the materials which were before the E . O . 
in the domestic enquiry . It appears that the management 
had examined 7 witnesses before the Enquiry Officer and 
the concerned workmen also examined five witnesses in 
their defence . The first charge against the concerned work 
men is under clause 10 (1 ) of the Standing Order Ext. 


The case of the management is that on receipt of a 
report from Shri R . C . Ram the two concerned workmen 
were issued with chargesheet dated 12- 3 - 84 by the Dy 
Chief Engineer ( C . O . ) Loyabad Coke Plant . The allegations 
constituted misconduct on the part of the concerned work 
orn within the meaning of . O . The two concerned work . 
men suhmitted their explanation . It was considered hy the 
Dy Chief Engineer who is the head of the establishment 
and found the explanation unsatisfactorv . He appointed 
Shri S . Klimar, Senior Personnel Officer Siiua Area as the 
Erairy Offirer. The Enquiry Officer after giving due 
notices to the concernen workmen held the enquiry . The 
witnesses of the management were examined in presence of 
the chargesheeted workmen and they were given full op 
portunity to cross - examine them . The concernod workmen 
were also given onnortunity to make their own statement 
and to examine their own witnesses in deferice . The en 
quiry was held in arcordance with the princinles of natu 
rat inestica . A11 moerihle reasonable annoriunities were 
giorning 10 the concerned workmen to defend themselves. The 
com erned workmen had also taken assistance of the co 


M - 15 which is in respect of wilful insubordination dis

or others, of any lawful or reasonable order of a superior . 
The Enquiry Officer in the enquiry report Ext. M - 12 in 
his conclusion at page 6 has discussed the charge under 
Clause 10 ( 1 ). It is stated that as per evidence of MWs, 
1 , 2 , 3 , 4 , 5 , and 6 the two concerned workmen were 
amongst the workers who were agitating talking in loud 
voices and were in high temper and the incidence disclosed 
that these two workers were ring leaders and they brought

Dy. C . M . E . and created disorderly scene and these acts 
of their amount to wilful insubordination by them as they 
had left the work during the working hours without per 
nusion of superiors . I have gone through carefully into 
the evidence of all the management s witness . The evidence 
do not disclose that the concerned workmen were leaders 
of the agitating workmen or that they had organised the 
work men for sic ! Anitation . Tt annears from the evidence 
that a groun of workmen were agitating for grant of leave

In the result, the reference is answered accordingly . This 
is my Award .

hunger strike on 7 - 3 - 84 and that they were pressing for 
grant of louve from the Dy. Chief Engineer, The manage 
quent s Witness No. 2 Shri D . Kimur has stated that the 
Area Cffice way contacted and the workmen were asked 
to keep yuiet and thereafter all the workmen dispersed at 
about 12 . 30 P . M . It will thus appear that the concerned 
worknica and the other workinen hud not gone on strike 
but bas merely collected for the grant of casual leave to 
the workmen who had gong on huoger strike on 7 - 3 - 84 . 
There is no cvicience to the effect that any order was given 
by the sliperior officer to the concerned workmen which 
they disclosed . Thus strictly spcaking the charge under 
clause 10 ( 1 ) of the Standing Orders of wilful insubur 
dination or disobedience of any lawful or a reasonable 
order of superior does not appear to have been established 
against the concerned workmen . 

It will appear that the concerned workmen along with 
others were agitating for the grant of casual leave for the 
Workinen who had gone on liungca strike. But there is no 
eviaence that they had completely struck work on that 
date , They had for sometime left the work and had ap 
proached the management s officçry for the grant of casual 
leave as the leave was not being granted by Shri R . C . Ram , 
Personnel Oncer. The loavo was actually granted when Shri 
Shahi an Officer from the Area Om o came und granted the 
leave . Thus the concerned workmen and others had assembled 
only to put their grievance for the grant of casual leave 
bofore higher officers and these act of their cannot be said 
to constitute strikc. There is no evidence that the concem 
ed workmen or other workmen had not returned to their 
duties after the alleged agitation before the Officers . They 
had, no doubt, left their work for sometime in order to 
represent their view point, before the officers in agitated 
state . But that docs not mean that they had gone on a 
srike. I hold , therefore , that here is no evidence before 
the Enquiry Officer to come to a conclusion that the con 
cerned workmen along with others had gone on a strike 
or that the concerned workmen had incited the workmen 
to go on strike, 

Clausc 1008 ) of the Standing Order s deals with drunk 
cnncss , fighting riotous disorderly or indecent behaviour 
on the factory premises . There is no evidence of drunken 
ness nor there is any allegation of drunkenness of the 
concerned workmen , There is evidence to the effect that 
onc of ihe workman was pushed by some other workmen to 
wards Shri R . C . Run but the person being pushed was 
stopped and nobody was hurt. There is no definite cvidence 
that the person pushed was one of thic concerned work 
men or that the concerned workmen had pushed the said 
person towards Shri R . C . Ram . There is absolutely no 
evidence to show that the concerned work men had tried 
to assault Shri R . C . Ram or any other person . The only 
eviderice which has come in the statement of some of the 
management s witnesses is that both the concerned work 
men lail filthily abused Shri R . C . Ran . MW - 1 Shri R . C . 
Ram . MW -2 D . Kumar , MW - 5 Shri A , K , Sinha, MW - 7

workmen had filthily abuscd Shri R . C . Ran MW - 3 Shri 
D . Gupta , MW - 4 Shri Chandrika Pd ., and MW -6 Shri 
Man Sirch have stated that abuses were being hurdlid on 
Shri R . C . Ran by the worknien but they have not spe 
cifically stated that they had seen or heard the two con 
cerned workmier abusing Shri R . C . Ram . It will appear 
from the evidencc adduced on behalf of the concerned 
worlimen also that Shri R . C . Ram hací protested before 
Shri Sahi that the workmen had abused him . Taking all these

13 . Learned Counsel for the workman las vehcmcntly con 
tended that the management has not given an opportunity 
before arriving at a decision . This is not correct. Ample 
opportunity for over five years was given to him to adduco 
some reliable evidence . He was given ample opportunity in 
departmental inatiera , Natural justice does not require to 
give opportunity at every stago of the proceedings in the 
file of the office. It has also been contended that no rcasons 
are assigned and the managerrent has not said that the 
School Certificate is forged , In departmental orders reasons 
at length are not required to be communicated to the work 
man . It is sufficent if the decision taken is communicated 
to him which has been done in the instant case . For the 
reasons stated above I am of the opinion that the action of 
the management was justified and the workman la not entitled 
to any relief.

We shall have first to consider the provisions of Sec. 25F , 
This section lays down certain conditions precedent 10 
retrenchmeat of a workman . The word " retrenchment" has 
been defined in Sec . 2100 ) and it covers every casc of 
termination by the employer of tho service of a workman 
for any reason whatsoever otherwisc than as a punishment 
int!icted by way of disciplinary action and except in certain 
cascs which are specifically mentioned therein . Shri Rathod 
for the workman concerned drew my attention to several 
decisions of the Hon ble Supreme Court with regard to 
termination of the service of workman amounting to 
retrenchment. Shri Rathod , in this connection , mainly 
relied on the decisions reported in 1976 - 1, L .LJ. 478 , 
1980 - II, LL ,J ., 72 , A . I. R , 1983 S . C . 1320 and A . I. R . 
1981 S . C . 1253. Reading the above decisions, it is very 
clear that every case of termination of service of a workman 
by an act of the enployer cxccpt those not expressly included 
in Scy . 23F or which are covered by the excepted categories 
mentioned in Sec. 2 ( 00 ) , would amount to " retrenchment" . 
The termination of the service in the present casc , admittedly , 
does not fall within any of the excopted categories. Thore 
fore , the termination of the service of the workman concerned 
would definitely be " retrcachment " and when it is " rctronch 
ment" the requirements of Soc . 25F have to be complicd 
with and if they are not complicd with , the termination 
would be void . It will, therefore , have to be seen whether 
in the present case theso requirements bave been complied 
with . But it is very important to note here that cvery 
case of retrenchment will not be hit by the provisions of 
S¢c . 25F inasmuch as in order to apply those provisions 
the workman who has been retrenched should have been 
in continuous service for not less than one year under the 
employer. Now , in regard to what would be " continuous 
srvice for not less than one ycar " , wo shall have to refer 
to Sec . 25B . Clause ( 1 ) of Sec . 25B states as to when a 
workmon shall be said to be in continuous service for a 
period . However , even if the workman is not in continuous 
service for a period of one your within the mcaning of 
clause ( 1), clause (2Xa) says that he shall be doomod to be 
in continuous service for a period of one year if he , during 
a period of twelve calendar months precoding the date with 
reference to which calculation is to be made , has actually 
workod under the employer for not less than 240 days . 
Then , Explanation to clause ( 2 ) mentions that for the pur 
poses of clause (2 ) the number of days on which a workman 
has actually workod under an employer shall include the 
days of four type: as covered in (i) to (tv ) therein . In the 
present case , there is no dispute that the workman concerned 
was with the Bank for in all 242 days . But the crucial laguc 
in whether he can be paid to have " actually - - worked" for 
these 242 days having regard to the provisions of Soc . 25B .

in Writ Petition Nos, 298 and 599 of 1975 in which Their 
Lordships of the Madras High Court were pleased to obyerve

counsel for the petition would contend that in

We are in cntire agreement with this contonțion of 
the learned counsel for the management. The 
words " actually worked " would not in lude even 
holidays for which full wages are paid . The words 
" days workçd " itself would normally mean day ! 
actually worked . The icgislature as if it were to 
give emphasis, has also added the words " actually " . 
There could therefore be no scope for argument 
that paid holidays are to be included in " actually 
worked " days . The explanation to Sec . 25 - B hus 
included in this " actually worked days ? certain 
" deemed actual working days" . Only these days 
which are provided in the explanation could be 
included in calculating the 240 days in addition 
to the actual working days and we cannot enlarge 
the meaning of the words “ actually worked " beyond 
what is contained in the explanation . The number 
of working days worked by a workman in a com 
pleted year of service thus will have to be worked 
out with reference to the actual working days if 

it is a case falling under clause (2 )." 
Thus , the important question which hag arisen in this 
case is whether for the purposes of computing 240 dayą 
Sundays and holidays should be taken into consideration or 
not. As per the Hon ble Madras High Court s decision , 
Sundays and holidays arc to be cxcluded . If that view is 
accepted and applicd to the present case , the workman 
concerncd cannot be said to have completed 240 days 
actual working and therefore he cannot be said to be in 
continuous service for a period of one year, which in turn 
would mean that the provisions of Sec . 25F would not apply 
in the present case and as such there would be no question 
of giving any notice or payment of wages in lieu of notice 
or any compensation and ultimately the action of termination 
of the services of the workman concerned would not be bad . 

7 . It is true that Their Lordships of the Madras High Court, 
in the above referred decision , were clcarly of the view that 
the legislature as if it were to give emphasis, has also added 
the words " actually and there could therefore be no scopo 
for argument that paid holidays are to be included in the ac 
tually worked days . According to Their Lordships of the 
Madras High Court, the Explanation to Sec . 25B har included 
in this " actually worked day , " certain " deemed actual work 
ing days " and only those days which are provided in the ex 
planation could he included in calculating the 240 days in 
addition to the actual working days and the meaning of the 
words " actually workeu " cannot be enlarged beyond what is 
contained in the explanation . I have given my cnxious 
thought to the decision aforesaid and with great respect, I 
am not in a position to subscribe to the vicws expressed by 
Their Lordships of the Hon bic Madras High Court, for the 
reason , firstly that whilo considering a social welfarc legis 
lation the Court should adopt a beneficent rule of construction 
and if a section is capable of twis constructions , that cons 
truction ghould be preforred which fulfils the policy of the 
Act, and is more beneficial to the persons in whose interost 
the Act has been ransed . Further , the principle of giving 
beneficial interpretation to a provision arises when two rca 
sonable interpretations of a pro : slon are possible and one of 
which is advantageous to the worknian and the other is not 
In such a case , the court should adopt one which favour 
the weaker section for whose benefit the enactment has been 
pared . In the instant case , the aneation turns on inclusion 
or erclusion of Sundays and holidays for the purtoro of 
calculatine 240 days . Now , If the construction put up by Their 
lordshire of the Madras High Court is adonted . I am afraid . 
many of the workmen working in the various industries would 
he affected adversely . Again , if we read the Explanation to 
clause ( 2 ) of Sec. 25R , it save " the niimber of davs on which 
a workman has actually worked under an emnlover shall in 
clude the days . . . . . . The word " shall include" would moan 
that the dave falline linder the four caterorier under (1 ) to 
( lv ) would not be the only days which should be treated 
" actually workod " days ( though the workload would not have 


Even the Learned Author N . S . Bindra in his Book " The 
Interpretation of Statutes " while dealing with this aspect, 
hay staled that when in an interpretation clause , it is stated 
that a certain term " includes " so and so , it implics that the 
term retained its ordinary meaning whatever it may mean . It 
hus only an extended forco and does not limit the meaning 
of the term to the substance of the definition . I am inclined 
lo agree with the above vicws and accordingly in my opinion 
also what the Explanation gays under items (i ) to (iv ) is not 
restrictive . My view iş fortified by item (ii ) of the Explana 
iton which states that the days on which a workman has been 
on leave with full wages , carned in the previous year are to 
be included in calculating the actually worked days . Again , 
if we look to Sec . 18 of the Bombay Shops and Establishments 
Act (which Act applied to the Bank at the relevant time) it 
iny s down that every shop and conimercial establishinent shall 
romain closed on one day of the week . Thus , cven a work 
man who is willing to work canot work and the employer 
also cannot give him work on vae day of a week . Now , if 
this non -working day which is a statutory requirement is to 
be excluded for the puropse of arriving at " two hundred and 
forty days" mentioned in item (li ) of sub -clause ( a ) of clauso 
( 2 ) of Sec . 25B , I do not think it is in any way justified . It 
is more so because while making computation about tho 
earned leave under the provisions of the Bombay Shops and 
Establishments Act, Sundays are included in the days worked , 
In any event, ia tho instant case , as we have seen , two inter 
prctations are possible and as stated above when two inter 
pretations are possible , the one which fayoun the persons in 
whose nterest the Act has been pagsed should be preferred . 
Therefore , in my opinion , Sundays and other statutory holi 
days should be included for the purpose of cakulating 240 
days envisaged in Sec , 25B . When this is dono, the workman 
concerned herein would be said to have actually worked under 
the employer viz ., tho Bank for not less than 240 days . Con 
sequently he will be deemed to be in continuous acrvice under 
the Bank for a period of one year. If that is so , under Sec . 
25F the workman concerned should firstly have been given 
One month s notice in writing indicating the reasons for re 
trenchment or wages for ono month in lieu of the notice , and 
secondly he should have been paid retrenchmcnt compensation 
us contemplated under that soction . Both these conditions 
should be fulfiled in order to make it a valid retrenchment. 
In the instant case , while it is contended on behalf of tho 
Bank that Fr. 62 should be treated as the notice , it is ad . 
mitted by the Bank that no retrenchment compensation has 
been paid or even been offered . The result would be that the 
action of terminating the sorvices of the workman concerned 
would amount to retrenchment and the retrenchment effected 
herein in bad in law and hence inoperative inasmuch as it is 
in violation of the provisions of Sec . 25F of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947.

mervices of the workmon concerned is bad and inoperative ,

in all cases to order reinstatement. In the case of Sbankar

2567 and 1982 bad held that mero non -compliadce with Stc 
tion 25F in non - payment of retrenchment compensation itself 
should not rult in the reinstatenient of the employec . Thus , 
it appears that reinstatement with payment of back wages is 
nou a rust in all cases . It was held by the Hon ble Madras 
High Court in Coimbatore Pioneer B Mils Ltd ., Vs. Labour 
(" ourt, Coimbatore and others ( writ Appeals Nos . 145 and 
146 of 1975 ) that the Labour Court ( or the Industrial Tri

concerned were terminated at the close of the business on 30th

These figures show that from 4th October, 1979 onwards the 
Workman concerned earned fairly well looking to his clerical 
job . Thus , considering all the various circumstances of this 
case , I would award to the workman concerned by way of 
compensation in lieu of reinstatement half the wages which 
he would have drawn bad he not been terminated . from the 
date of his termination till the dato he got gainful cmploy 
ment viz . till 3 - 10 - 1979 . Of course , normally compensation of 
this nature is given from the date of termination till the date 
of the Award. However , in the instant case , I am advisedly 
directing grant of half the wages as compensation from the 
date of termination till the date he secured gainful employ 
ment, vix . 3 - 10 - 1979. This is because generally the wages 
which a workman has carned out of his gainful employment 
arc deducted from the total amout of compensation granted 
but if that formula is adopted in the present case , the work 
man concerned is likely to be at a disadvantage inasmuch as 
he has earned fairly good wages in his new employment. It 
is because of this reason that directions are given as above . 


9 . From the above stated decisions , it clearly appears that , 
the tribunal has a discretion , on the facts and circumstances 
of a case , either to grant reinstatement or to grant compensa 
tion in lieu of reinstatement, or both . Considering now thic 
facts of the present caso , It has come in evidence that the 
workman cançrned raised an industrial dispute , for the first 
time, on 4 - 8 - 1979 and a reference was made on 16 - 7 - 1980 . 
The date of termiation of the workman concerned is 1 - 10 - 1973 
which means that the dispute was raisod after ncarly 5 years 
or so . It has also to be borne in mind that he has been in 
gainful employment in a Government department since 
4 - 10 - 1979 . It is also in evidence that though he has alleged 
that he hag passed the MIBM test, he has not been able to 
prove it. On the contrary , the list of successful candidates pro 
ducod by the Bank shows that the workman concerned has 
not been declarod guccessful at the said test. It has also to 
be noted that today the Bank cannot take any temporary hand 
as per the rules and regulations prevailing and the Bank also 
cannot employ a person who has not passed the MIBM test , 
It is also to be noted that in case his reinstatement is ordered 
hr will have to be placed on his original past with continuity 
of service which would again create several questions of 
soniority , etc . As against all these , the workman concerned 
has been gainfully employed in Government service for tho 
last about 6 years and he is well settled in his job . In mv 
opinion , therefore , looking to the circumngtances obtaining in 
This case , it would not be advisable to direct reinstatement of 
the workman concerned , and the ends of justice would be met 
if sono suitable compensation in lieu thereof is awarded . 


11 . For the reasons stated above, it is directed that the 
Bank shall pay to the workman concerned by way of com 
pensation , in lieu of reinstatcment, half the wage , which he 
would have earned had he not been terminatod , from tho dato 
of his termiation to the date he got gainfu ] einployment, viz 
from 1 - 10 - 1973 to 3 - 10 - 1979 . The Bank shall also pay a sum 
of Rs. 300 to the workman concerned by way of costs .

3 . The case of the management is that the workman , Shri 
Indra Behari, was found drinving the Ambulance inside the 
Faciory in a rash manner under the influence of alcohol 
which was verified by the Medical Examination . He was 
placed under suspension on 10 - 10 - 1981 and subsequently 
charge -sheeted under Rule 14 of C . C . S . ( C .C . A .) Rules 
1955. He admitted the charge vide his written statement of 
defence dated 20 - 11 - 1981 Since this was his second offence 
o drunkenness on duty penalty prescribed by Ordnance 
Factory Board s letter No. 567 | A Vig . dated 29 -4 - 1973 tte 
removal from service was awarded to him . However , the 
workman died on 4th October , 1983 due to heart failure 
even before this reference was made on 16 - 7 - 1984 . Hence 
his legal representatives viz . widow , daugther and mother 
appeared on his behalf.

4 . The factum of his death is supported by an Affidavit 
dated . 22 - 9 - 1984 of his father , Shri Shankar Lal Verma . 
The management raised a preliminary objection that since 
Shri Indra Behari died before the relerence this reference is 
not tenable and be rejected . 


5 . I have gone through the recorded and considered the 
law applicable in such cases. It is pertient to note in this 
case that this dispute was between the workman , Shri Indra 
Behari, and the management in his induividual capacity . It 
was not a collejective dispute . Law is well settled now that 
where it is an individual dispute with the death of the 
individual workman concerned the dispute lapses and where 
it is a collective dispute it continues to exist after the death 
of the one individual workman ; and where the dispute is 
concerning one workman but that is to effect the status of 
successor in office and in that case the industrial dispute 
subsists even after the death of the individual workman as 
has been held in Bihar Working Journalists Union Vs. H . R . 
Chowdhury and another ( 1968 Lab . I. C 515 ). 


6 . Another aspect of the matter is that in the instant 
case the dispute is whether the removal of Shri Indra Behari 
from service with effect from 1 - 12- 1981 is justified . In 
other words if found unjustified he will be entitled to re 
instatement which relief cannot be awarded since he died 
even before the reference to this Tribunal. 


New Delhi, the 14th October, 1985 
S . O . 4980.- In pursuance of section 17 of the Indus 
trial Disputes Act, 1947 ( 14 of 19 47 ) , the Central Govern . 
ment hereby publishes the following award of the Central 
Government Industrial Tribunal, Jabalpur, as shown in the 
Annexure in the industrial dispute between the employers 
in relation to the management of Gun Carriage Facstory, 
Jabalpur and their workmen , which was received by the 
Central Government on the 3rd October, 1985.

order of removal from service passed by the General Man 
ager , dated 1- 12 - 1981 : - -- 


" On careful consideration of the written statement of 

defence to memorandum referred to above , where 
in the Govt. employee has admitted the charge ,

AWARD 
Dated September, 22th October, 1985 
By Notification No. L . 14012(3 ) /83 - D . II ( B ) , dated 
16 - 7 - 1984 the Central Government has referred the follow 
ing dispute for adjudication ; 
" Whether the action of the management of Gun Cari 

riage Factory , Jabalpur in removing Sri Indra 
Behari, Labour B . T . No . 5388 LE from service 
· with effect from 1 - 12 -81 is justified ? If not, 10 

what relief is the workman entitled ?" 
2 . The case of the application is that late Shri Indra 
Behari was appointed as Labour B at the Gun Carriage 
Factory , Jabalpur on 28 - 9 - 1972 and he joined his duties on 
30 - 9 - 1972 . He was working in M . T . Section . It is further 
alleged that without any enquiry or notice he was removed 
from service vide Factory Order dated 22 - 12 - 1981 which 
was illegal and against natural justice . The matter went up 
to the Central Government and hence this reference . 


Thus on merits also the deceased workman or his legal 
representatives are not entitled to succeed . For the reasons 
discussed above I find on account of the death of the work 
man , Shri Indra Behari , even before the reference was made 
the reference becomes infructuous. In the absence of any 
evidence on behalf of the workman I hold that looking to 
the nature of the charge against him the action of the 
management of G . C . Factory Jabalpur in service Shri Indra 
Behari, Labour B T . No . 5388 / IE from service with effect 
from 1 - 12 - 1981 is justified . As such his legal representatives 
are not entitled to any relief.

17 . Now coming to the letter Ext. M -4 which purported 
to have been written by the Branch Manager Sri Ramesh 
Rao , certain facts are necessary to find out the genuineness 
of this document. It is suggested to WW - 1, the General 
Secretary that the said Ramesh Rao is now facing a criminal 
prosecution and C . B . I. investigation with regard to the mis 
appropriation and other charges. It is further elicited that 
this witness will not remember whether he has received Ext. 
W -4 personally or by post and he does not remember whe

or not and he has not informed the Bank in any of the

18 . On a perusal of Ext. W - 4 it is it typed letter on a 
plain paper addressed to the name of General Secretary nar 
rating the apology vide letter dated 30 - 5 -83 and certain 
difficulties undergone by this workman and his recommenda 
tion mudo to the General Manager on 30 -5 -83 over the tele 
phone not to take any action on the resignation letter und 
to transfer the workman to Mysorc City due to his difficulty . 
This witness being a General Secretary of the Union was 
not able to say the manner in which this letter was received 
hy him and the occasion for sending such a letter to his 
address without any specific request made by him . Though 
this letter is dated 12 -6 -83 the fact that he has not been 
brought to the notice of the General Manager vido his letter 
Ext. W - 2 dated 21- 5 - 83 clearly shows that this letter is a 
got up document invented for the purpose of this case . I 
have compared the signature of M . Ramesh Rao signed in 
Ext. M - 1 which is an admitted signature with the signature 
in Ext. W -4 which is a disputed letter and though therc is 
some similarity in the disputed letter to that of admitted 
Jetter, in view of the information that the said Ramesh Rao 
is now facing criminal prosecution there is every possibility 
of creating a document of this nature with the connivance 
of the said Ramesh Rao for the purpose of this case cannot 
be ruled out. When the genuineness of this letter was in 
question , the I Party have not been prevented to examine the 
author of this letter by taking slimmons of this Tribunal if 
he is not in service or requesting the Tribunal to direct the 
IT Party to make available before this Tribunal if he is still 
in service . Hence this letter cannot be relied for any purpose .

21, On a scrutiny of the oral documentary evidence it 
did reveal that the workman has tenderded his resignation

25 . The learned counsel on both sides have interpretod 
Rule 522 ( 2 ) and ( 3 ) of Sastry Award in support of the stand 
taken by them . The counsel for the JI Party has submitted 
that once the resignation is tendered and accepted beforo 
its withdrawal the resignation will ( vercome final. The learn 
ed counsel went a step further and submitted that once it is 
accepted the communication is not important. Against this 
submission , the learned counsel for the I Party has submitted 
that the acceptance of the resignation is not complete until 
the party is relieved by giving a relief letter . 


26 . Rule 522 of the Sastry Award proscribed the proceduro 
for termination of employment. Under sub -clause ( 2 ) It is 
stated that a permanent employeo desirous of leaving the 
service of the Bank shall give one month g notice in writing 

. . . ... . .when he leaves service, by given an order of relief 
signed by the manager . Sub -clause ( 3 ) contemplates that if 
anv permanent emplovee leaves the service without giving 
notice he shall be liable to pay the Bank one month s pay 
and allowances . 


22. It further discloses that the receipt of Ext. M - 5 dated

as in the column “ Particulars of message" the name of this

27. On a reading of this provision it does not show when 
the resignation tendered by an employee will become final 
but it only shows the procedure that has to be adopted in 
the case of accepting the resignation and it is left to tho 
bank to collect one month s " ulary in case a resignation in 
tendered without one month s notice or waive it. Since it 
is the discretion of the management to collect one month s 
wages, non - collection of the same will not render the accep 
tance of the resignation invalid . It should be borne in mind 
that after Ext. M - 4 that the acceptance of resignatiou no 
further communication appears to have made to the work 
man with regard to the relief order and settlement of account, 
This action is not at all relevant in th s cusc in view of the 
developnients that took place and the whole matter wag

30 - 5 -83.

33 . In Union of India vs. 0 . C . Misra reported in 1978 
SC I L .L . J 492 , the Full Bench of Supreme Court, Ilis lord 
Ahir Murtaza Fazal Ali, dissenting, have held hat a l esignal 
tion letter effective from a future dutc can be withdiau n 
before that date is reached . In this case , a High Court Judge 
has sent a resignation letter dated 7 - 5 -77 addressed 10 the 
President resigning Judgeship from 1 - 8 -77. Meanwhile , the 
Judge changing his mind sent another letter dated 15- 7 - 77 
withdrawing his curlier letter and joining the Bench . Their 
Lordshing further held that his letter dated 7 - 5 -77 was only 
a proposal or notice to resign on a future date and not being 
an absolute and complete resignation operative with immedi 
ate effect could be and , in fact, had been validly withdrawan 
by a subsequent letter dated 15- 7 - 77. 


The acceptance of resignation lelier dated 28 - 5 - 83 vide 
the II Party s letter duled 4 -6 - 83 is liercby set aside und 
the 11 Party -management is dire ted to reinstate the i Party 
workman from the date of publication of this award in the 
post which he was holding on The Jate of his resignation 
letter without disturbing the cmoluincols he was draving as 
on that date . The Party workman is not entilled for any 
consequential benefits, such as, back wages , cost of this 
litigation , etc . The parlies shall bear their own costs .

Leaving Certificate . I think that it is a case of gcnuine diſe 
rence of a ve recorded in the records of the managerunt. Fxt. 
M - 1 is the circular of TBCCI dated 5 - 2 -85 . The procedure of 
determination of date of birth in respect of existing emplove 
cs is stated in para - B at page 3 of the suid circular : In sub 
paril 1 ( a ) it is stated that wherever there is no variation 
in records, such cases will not be reopened unless there is 
a very glaring and apparent wrong entry brought to the 
notice of the managemont. The management after theing 
satisfied on the merits of the case will take andronrinta notion 
for correction through age determination committee Medical 
Board . It is stated that wherever there are variations a 
suitahle provisio for agc determination comittee Medical

annuation of the concerned workman .

SCHEDULE 
" Whether the action of the management of Vijaya Bank 

in relation to its Hospet Branch in not providing 
employment beyond February , 19 , 1983 to Shri 
M , Nagarajuchar, Peon is justified ? If not , to what 

relief is the workman concerned entitled ? " 
2 . The above dispute was registered and notices were sent 
tu both the parties and on behalf of the II Party Sri K . J . 
Alvil , advocate has filed the vakaluth and on behalf of the 
I party onç Sri M . C . Narasimhan , an advocate has under 
taken to appear on 15th October , 1984 . In spite of 8 ad 
journments the I Party has failcd to appear and he has also 
not filed any statement in support of the dispute . In view 
of this the I Party treated as cx - parte and directed the si 
Pariy to file their statement. Since no statement has been 
filed for a considerable period this Tribunal has suo -moto 
called the conciliation file relating to this dispute and imme 
diately the receipt of the same, the II Party has filed their 
statement showing the justification in not providing the 
employment to the I Party workman , 

3 . The Il Party have contended in their statement that 
the l Party was engaged by the Hospet Branch against the 
intermittent leave vacancies of the permanent peons between 
1976 to 1983. They have not engaged in services of the 1 
Party workman more than 65 days continuously in any 
period . They have further contended as per the directive, 
the appointinent of the sub -ordinate cadre shall be made 
through Employment Exchange. Accordingly , the vacancy 
was notified and since the name of the I Party workman 
was not sponsored by the Employment Exchange, the bank 
could not consider his case for appointment. It is furtlicr 
contended that they have not terminated the services of the 
I Putty because he has crossed 25 years of age. The nature 
of job is temporary and on completion of the process of 
appointment the service of l Party was not required . There 
is no obligation on their part to absorb the I Party for per 
manent basis. The bipartite settlement not , provides for giv 
ing preference to temporary employee in filling up thc per 
mancnt vacancies if they are otherwise eligible and fulll the 
conditions stipulated for recruitment. Thc II Party has the 
right to terminate temporary pcons as per terms of contract. 
They hold that they are justified in terminating the acrvices 
of the I Party .

" Whether the management of M / s. Western Coalfields 

Limited , in Rajur Colliery in Sub - Area No. 1, P . O . 
Rajur , Distt. Yeotmal (MS) is justified in dismiss 
ing the services of the workman Shri Subhash 
Chandra Pandey Under Ground Trammer with 
effect from 20th December, 1983 ? If not, to what 
relief the workman is entitled ? " 


6 . Since the I Party workman has failed to place any 
material in support of his claim for the continuation of his 
employment or alternatively to considering for the perman 
nent post, I am compelled to pass the following award . 


AWARD 


2 . The incident giving rise to the charge sheet and the subse 
quent enquiry is alleged to have occured on 10th July , 1983 
at Rujur Colliery of W . C .L ., about mid - night. Indictment 
against the workman is that at the relevant time he assaulted 
Shri Sukhmai Saha, Trammer, who was working as a Pit 
Munshi and beat him with the helt , causing him injuries . 
When the matter was reported to the superiors a charge sheet 
was served on the workman on 11 / 12 - 7 - 1983 on the strength 
of which thc enquiry proceeded . It seems that the workman 
wanted to appoint Shri D . N . Chobe as his dcfenco 
representative but since he was not serving in Rajur Col 
liery but in the Majri Collicry an objection was taken for 
his appointment by the management as a result of which 
Shri Vijay Bahadur Singh represented the defence . How 
over the record shows that the matter was conducted by 
the workman himself who cross -examined all the manage 
ment witnesses. 


The action of the management of Vijaya Bank in rela 
tion to its Hospet Branch in not providing employment be 
yond February 19 , 1983 to Shri M . Nagarajachar , Peon is 
justified . The workman is not entitled for any relief. No 
Order as to costs .

" Whether the action of the management of Ma. Wortera 

Coalfields Limited , Hindustan Lalpeth Colliery 
P . O . & Di$ it. Chandrapur in relation to Hindustan 
Lalpoin Colliery of Sub Arda No. 3 , Wardha Valiey 
Alcu in refusing to change the date of birth of Shri 
Pandhari Reghunath , Electric Pump Khalasi is justi 
fied ? If not , to what relief the said worknian is 
entitled ?" 


2 . As the dispute stands , it centrus around the question 
us to which 18 tho correct year of birth whether 1924 as 
recorded in the records of the colueries or 1929 as stated 
by the workmen . This assun . us importance because as per 
the standing orders thc workman is entitled to continue uplo 
60 years of age and if his correct date of birth is found 
to be 20 - 2 - 1929 as alleged , he would be entitlod to continuo 
upto 29 - 2 - 1989 against which he was made to retire in 
1984 on the strength of the year of birth as 1924 . 


11. As the workman has been dismissed from service the 
question is whether the punishment of dismissal is barsh or 
disproportionale . If the workman felt aggrieved by the 
action of the Ventillation Officer, he having marked him ab 
sent, he should have approached the superior officer when 
be found Sliri Sarkar to be adamant but should never hayo 
taken the law in his hand and taken revenge . He pushed the 
Ventillation Officer af a result of which he fell on the 
ground, he threatened to drink his blood and 
cych tried to hurle brick bat at him , This hat 
Occured in the office where Shri Sarkar was working. In 
these circumstanjes in the light of the misconduct ag proved , 
if ile management decided to part with his companay , dis 
missing him th > order of dismissal can never be said to bo 
harsh or disproportionate. The management has to maintain 
discipline and if such type of incidents which losult in grave 
consequences arc ignored or tolerated , chaog is bound to 
case. I therefore hold that the order of dismissal dated 
16 - 12 - 1981 is justificd and the workman is act entitled to 
any relief. 
Award accordingly.

By their order No. L -22012 ( 13 ) |84-D . V , dated 26 -3 - 1985 
the following dispute hag been roferred for adjudication under 
Section 10 ( 1 ) (d ) of the Industrial Disputer Act, 1947: 


REASONS 


6 . The only cruciar question arising for determination is 
whether the correct birth date of the applicant is 1- 7 - 1924 
as shown in the record or 20- 2 - 1929 as alleged by the work 
man and in support of which he has produced extract of the 
Birth Register Ex . W - 3 showing that on 20 - 2 - 1929 a malo 
son was born to Shri Ragya Mahar . In the same entry in 
column No. 2 , there is the name of Budhia appearing and 
the quostion is to whom this entry refers . The management 
has produced on iecord at Ex. M - 4 an Extract of Birth 
Register of the same village showing various entries from 
which we find that in column No. 2 besides ihe name of 
father, in the samo entric tho pano of child , whether mado 
or femalo is also stated . If this practico is taken into account 
and which is evident from the entries from Ex. M - 4 , it clearly 


[ erre II - 


5683 


WTCT * 7 


3 ( ii ) ] 


: 9 


4, 1907 


26, 1985/# / fa 


indicate , the name of Budhis appearing in the extract prodno 
ed by the workman is the name of the male child born to 
Shri Ragya Mahar op 20 - 2 - 1929 and he must be different 
frors the workman in question i.e . he must be the brother 
of the workman , This shows that no reliance can be 
placed on Ex. W - 3 since the entry does not appear to be 
pertaining to the birth of the workman himself and once 
we arrive at this conclusion there is nothing to hold that the 
birth date mentioned in the record is erroneous one without 
which finding no relief is possible .

hay no ground for demanding his promotion as he is inych

and as such cannot demand to be posted as Senior Watcher 
in another cadre . The promotion is the management s func

The concerned workman had not been selected for his

not be made adversely without giving him notice under 
Section 9A of the I. D . Act, Ext. M - 7 shows that the con 
cerned workman was to be on probation for a period of 
one year which may be extended or curtailed at the discre 
tion of the management. There is no evidence of either ex 
tension or curtailment of the period of probation and as such 
the concerned workman had become permanent as Senior 
Watcher after completing one year of his service as a Senior 
Weather. In this view of the matter also a notice under 
Section 9A of the 1. D . Act was a must prior to the ieler 
sion of the concerned workman to Clerical Grade- II. 

It is admitted in the W . S . of the management that the 
concerned workman was not paid the wages for , Clerical 
Grade- I meaning thereby that the concerned workman had 
not been paid the wages as stated in Ext. M -7 . 

it is stated on behalf of the inanagement that there was 
a favouritism done by Shri D . P . S . Chouhan in appointing the 
concerned workman as Senior Watcher. Ext. M - 7 by which 
the concerned workman was appointed as a Senior Wat her 
was issued by the Dy . Chief Personnel Manager with the 
approval of the competent authority and there is nothing 
on the record to show that Shri D . P . S . Chouhan had shown 
favouritism in appointing the concerned workman as Senior 
Watcher . The management has not produced any docurient 
to show that any favouritism was shown in the garb of the 
plea that all documents relating to it were filed with the 
CBT. In order to prove favouritism the management had to 
establish the same and a mere verbal statement will not be 
enough to show that favoritism was shown in the appoint 
ment of Senior Watchers. 

In view of the discussion made above I hold that the 
demand of the workmen of Security headquarters of M / s . 
BCC Ltd. that Shri R . K . Singh should be promoted as 
Senior Watcher and placed in the scale of pay of Clerical 
Grade - I with effect from 21- 1 - 82 is justified and that the 
reversion of the concerned workman by the management is 
not ott 11 iustified because of the reasons discussed above . 
Admit :edly the concerned workman had worked as Serior 
Watcher in the scale of pay of Rs. 572 - 944 which is egitiva 
lent to the pay scale of Clerical Grade - I and as such the 
concerned workman is entitled to the said scale of pay from 
21 - 1 - 82 till 4 - 7 - 83 . As I have held above that the reversion 
of the concerned workman from the post of Senior Watcher 
to Clerical Grade - II is not justified , the con erned workman 
is reinstated as Senior Watcher with effect from 5 - 7 -83 ard 
is entitled to the back wages in the scale of Rs. 572 -- 94 .1 
per month with other consequential benefits 
This is my Award . 

I. N . SINHA , Presiding Officer 

¡No. L -24012 (42 ) 83 - D . IV ( B )] 
R . K . GUPTA , Desk Officer.

S . O . 4990 . - In exercise of the powers conferred by sub 
rule (2 ) of rule 13 of the Life Insurarce Corporation of 
India Class III and Class IV Employees (Revision of Terms 
and Conditions of Service ) Rules , 1985 , the Central Govern 
ment hereby determine that, subject to the other provisions 
of the said sub -rule , the payment in lieu of bonus for the 
period commencing on the 1st day of April, 1984 and ending 
with the 31st day of March , 1985 to every Class III and 
Class IV employee shall be at the rate of 15 ner cent of his 
salary.
